  ## The problem (TL;DR — full detail in the plan doc)                          
  TotalReclaw registers as OpenClaw's native `kind:memory` provider             
  (`memory_search`/`memory_get` via `registerMemoryCapability`). The provider   
  only ACTIVATES (tools bind, recall works) when `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json`    
  has `plugins.slots.memory = "totalreclaw"`. Two failure modes keep breaking   
  this:                                                                         
                                                                                
  1. **Slot reset** — OpenClaw resets `plugins.slots.memory` to `memory-core`   
  (disabled) after install/restart/hot-reload → no provider →                   
  `memory_search`/`memory_get` never bind → recall dead.                        
  2. **Install-record chicken-and-egg** — non-interactive CLI installs write    
  `installs.totalreclaw.version=None` → OpenClaw won't load the plugin → the    
  plugin-side self-heal can't fire.                                             
                                                                                
  rc.18 tried `patchOpenClawConfig` at `register()` (writes slot at boot) →     
  failed (OpenClaw strips the slot after register). rc.19 (#77) added a         
  `recheckSlot` hook to the trajectory poller → **unit test passes but it does  
  NOT work in production** (plugin loaded + record intact + slot set to         
  `memory-core` → not healed after 70s; no recheckSlot log lines; the dist IS   
  confirmed to contain the code + the poller IS running). The runtime gap is    
  unknown — that's Phase 0 of your investigation.                               
                                                                                
  **Likely real root cause (prioritize investigating this — plan "Approach      
  C1"):** OpenClaw auto-slots its built-in `memory-core` as the `kind:memory`   
  provider but does NOT auto-slot TotalReclaw, despite TR's                     
  `openclaw.plugin.json` declaring `kind:"memory"` +                            
  `memory_search`/`memory_get`. If TR's manifest declaration is subtly wrong    
  (or incomplete vs how memory-core declares itself), fixing it could make      
  OpenClaw auto-slot TR on every load — **eliminating the fragile reactive      
  self-heal entirely.** That is the "make it stable" path, not another          
  self-heal patch. Compare TR's `openclaw.plugin.json` against OpenClaw's       
  `memory-core` reference plugin (inside the container at                       
  `/app/dist/extensions/memory-core/`, or the 2026.6.8 source).

  ## How to test (the real gate — pop-os)                                       
  1. **Reinstall rc.X via the agent over Telegram** (interactive — Pedro sends: 
  "uninstall totalreclaw, install @totalreclaw/totalreclaw@rc.X, keep           
  credentials, restart"). Avoids the `--force` artifact.                        
  2. **Slot self-heal:** after reinstall+restart, `slots.memory` must =         
  `totalreclaw` with NO manual edit. Watch the file log for the heal.           
  3. **H1 (recall):** Telegram the agent "what do you remember about            
  TotalReclaw?" → returns real vault facts.                                     
  4. **#78 AA10 (write):** ~5 messages → extraction+on-chain write → file log   
  shows a landed UserOp, NOT `AA10 sender already constructed`.                 
  5. **Soak:** leave it; confirm slot still `totalreclaw` after hours + a       
  couple restarts.                                                              
  Only if all pass → publish rc.X → auto-QA → (eventually) stable-promote       
  (Pedro's gate).                                                               
                                                                                
  ## Constraints — NON-NEGOTIABLE                                               
  - **Phrase-safety:** NEVER modify                                             
  crypto/phrase/mnemonic/credentials/Smart-Account-derivation/key-material. The 
  recovery phrase must NEVER cross LLM context. (`project_phrase_safety_rule`;  
  CLAUDE.md.)                                                                   
  - **Scanner-clean:** no new `process.env`/`fetch`/`require`/`child_process`   
  markers in non-relay files — the scanner scans COMMENTS too.                  
  - **Blind relay:** all crypto is client-side; the relay is zero-knowledge —   
  never move key material to the relay.                                         
  - **Behavior-preserving** on happy paths; **lockstep** (`contracts.tools`     
  registered⊆declared) if you touch the manifest.                               
  - **Don't promote rc.19** (it's NO-GO). Don't ship a new RC until the pop-os  
  test (steps 1–5) passes.

  restore on failure; never touch `credentials.json`.

⏺ This fundamentally reframes the problem. The subagent's source trace
  establishes three corrections to the prior mental model:

  1. The slot reset is NOT automatic on startup/restart — it only happens under
  openclaw doctor --fix/--repair/--yes (maybeRepairStalePluginConfig sets the
  slot to the hardcoded default memory-core). Plain boot only scans+warns.
  2. knownIds is gated by on-disk manifest discoverability + install-path 
  provenance, NOT the version field — so "populate version" (prior Approach B)
  wouldn't fix loading; the real gate is disk-discoverability.
  3. Install already has a native slot-selection phase
  (applyExclusiveSlotSelection via plugins enable / install-persist) that sets
  slots.memory=totalreclaw AND disables memory-core — the proper mechanism TR is
  bypassing with hand-patching.

  And critically: C1 (manifest auto-slotting) is provably dead — OpenClaw
  hardcodes DEFAULT_SLOT_BY_KEY.memory="memory-core" and only ever assigns a
  non-default owner via explicit applyExclusiveSlotSelection. No manifest field
  triggers auto-preference.

⏺ Two decisive confirmations:

  1. The install-persist slot-selection phase runs unconditionally for any

  of TR should set slots.memory=totalreclaw and disable memory-core via

  2. The slot-reset code (repair-sequencing) is imported ONLY by

⏺ I now have a complete, source-grounded root-cause model — enough to reframe
  the plan and bring Pedro the decisions that actually shape it. Let me confirm
  the last mechanism I checked: applySlotSelectionForPlugin looks the plugin up
  in the slot-selection registry and silently no-ops if the plugin isn't found 
  or has no kind — which is exactly what a broken --force install produces. That
  closes the loop on both failure modes.

  Here's what I've established, then two decisions I need from you.

  ---
  Reframed root cause (from the actual 2026.6.8 host source — corrects the prior
  plan)

  I pulled openclaw@2026.6.8 and read the slot machinery. Three findings
  overturn the prior mental model:

  1. Approach C1 (manifest auto-slotting) is provably dead. OpenClaw hardcodes
  DEFAULT_SLOT_BY_KEY.memory = "memory-core" (slots.ts). There is no "auto-slot
  any kind:memory plugin" behavior. A non-default plugin owns the memory slot
  only via an explicit applyExclusiveSlotSelection call. TR's manifest is
  already correct (kind:"memory", contracts.tools:[memory_search,memory_get],
  activation.onStartup:false) — byte-for-byte equivalent to memory-core's. No
  manifest tweak can make OpenClaw prefer TR. So we should not chase C1.

  2. The slot only resets under openclaw doctor --fix — not on install, restart,
  or hot-reload. The reset code (maybeRepairStalePluginConfig → slots.memory = 
  defaultSlotIdForKey("memory") = "memory-core") is imported only by the doctor
  repair flow, gated behind --fix/--repair/--yes. Plain boot only scans + warns.
  This means the prior "OpenClaw strips the slot after every restart" model is
  wrong — something is running an apply-mode repair, or the slot was simply
  never set.

  3. A normal install already sets the slot natively. persistPluginInstall runs
  an unconditional "slot selection" phase for any enabled install →
  applySlotSelectionForPlugin("totalreclaw") → sets slots.memory=totalreclaw and
  disables memory-core. But it silently no-ops if TR isn't found in the
  registry or has no resolvable kind — i.e. when the install record is 
  broken/non-discoverable (the --force/piped version=None case). And knownIds is
  gated by on-disk discoverability + install-path provenance, not the version 
  field — so the prior Approach B ("populate version") wouldn't have fixed
  loading.

  The unified model

  - Agent-driven interactive install → complete, discoverable record → TR in
  registry with kind:memory → OpenClaw's own slot-selection sets
  slots.memory=totalreclaw + disables memory-core. Works with zero self-heal.
  - --force/piped install → broken record → TR not discoverable → slot-selection
  no-ops → slot stays memory-core; and TR ∉ knownIds so any later doctor --fix
  would strip a manually-set slot. Failure mode 1 is a consequence of failure
  mode 2.

  Implication: rc.18/rc.19's hand-patching + poller self-heal is a 2026.5.x-era
  workaround that, on 2026.6.8, is redundant on the good path and cannot win on
  the broken path (it fights discoverability, not the slot value). The stable
  fix is likely stop hand-patching, lean on OpenClaw's native slot-selection, 
  and ensure a discoverable install record — not another reactive patch.

  ⚠️ These are source-derived; Phase 0/1 on pop-os will confirm them on the live
  host before I touch code.

  Two decisions gate the refined plan:

  Shape — confirm-first, then surgical removal, then the real gate

  Phase 0 — CONFIRM native slot-selection on pop-os (hard gate).
  Neutralize TR's hand-patch, do a clean agent-driven reinstall, and verify
  OpenClaw's own install "slot selection" phase sets slots.memory=totalreclaw +

  Phase 1 — protect behavior. Confirm hooks.allowConversationAccess (Fix #2 —
  what makes extraction/recall hooks fire) is still needed on 2026.6.8, so I
  don't accidentally kill recall. Document --force/piped as a QA footgun (no
  code).

  Phase 2 — TDD removal (worktree off the rc.19 tag). Scope is precise:
  - ❌ Remove: patchOpenClawConfig Fix #1 (the slots.memory hand-write,
  fs-helpers.ts:1113–1157) + the recheckSlot poller hook (index.ts:5247 + the
  recheckSlot? dep in trajectory-poller.ts).
  - ✅ Keep: Fix #2/#3/#4 (hooks / telegram streaming / bundledDiscovery), the
  manifest (unchanged), and the SIGUSR1 restart if any kept fix still writes on
  first boot.
  - No new poller. At most a one-shot idempotent assert — and only if Phase 0
  surfaces a gap (default: don't add it).

  slot correctly on a clean install (proven from its source), and the slot only

  workaround eliminates the race that broke twice.

⏺ Monitor armed on the write path. Here's where we stand:

  Gate 4 root cause: zombie plugin instances, not rc.20

  The finding: the gateway process was 4d18h old — every "restart" since June 30
  was in-process (SIGUSR1). So rc.18, rc.19, and rc.20-qa.1 were all alive
  simultaneously in that one process, each with running pollers. The escaping
  AA10 errors carry rc.18's signature bug (#78 — sponsor call outside the retry
  loop + stale deployed-cache attaching initCode), and the concurrent
  same-content extractions (two different phrasings, 5s apart) + AA25 nonce
  races were the zoo fighting itself. rc.20's own retry loop structurally cannot
  leak an AA10 the way we observed.

  Action taken: restarted the container → clean single-instance boot (3 plugins,
  11.4s), and the slot survived the full process restart untouched — that's
  gate-5 restart-survival evidence, exactly as the doctor-gated-reset source
  finding predicted.

  Real rc.20-line bugs this exposed (fix before publish):
  1. TR never stops its poller on re-register — and TR's own SIGUSR1
  self-restart guarantees accumulation (today's install alone spawned 3
  same-version pollers). Needs a module-level singleton guard + a look at
  whether OpenClaw offers a dispose hook.
  2. Retry-loop silent exhaustion — after 2 failed attempts it exits without
  throwing, leaving userOpHash undefined → 120s zombie receipt poll → misleading
  error. Should rethrow the last error.
  3. (Latent) getInitCode reads an RPC error envelope as "not deployed" — should
  distinguish error from empty code.

  👉 Your move — gate 4 traffic

  Send ~5 casual Telegram messages to MBP-POP-Claw (drop in a memorable fact or
  two, e.g. what you had for dinner). The monitor will catch the extraction →
  UserOp submission live, and we'll see whether the clean process writes
  on-chain without AA10.

  While that brews, I'll implement fixes 1+2 on the branch (TDD).

⏺ rc.20-qa.2 tarball is built and staged on pop-os
  (/home/pdiogo/.openclaw/tr-qa-rc20-2.tgz — includes the poller lifecycle guard
  + write-path fixes). Review agent is going over Tasks 8–10 now.

  Your SPA question — persistent sign-in across refresh

  Yes, fixable — with one invariant we can't compromise: only derived keys may 
  ever touch storage, never the mnemonic (Keeper's mnemonic-never-at-rest
  design). Three tiers, by exposure:

  Option: A. sessionStorage for the derived vault key
  Survives: refresh (same tab); cleared on tab close
  Exposure: key readable by JS on the origin, but only for the tab's lifetime
  Effort: small — kills exactly the annoyance you hit
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: B. IndexedDB wrapped-key + auto-lock TTL
  Survives: refresh + browser restart, until TTL (e.g. 24h) or explicit Lock
  Exposure: XChaCha20 key stored wrapped by a non-extractable WebCrypto AES key;

    XSS can use it while unlocked but can't exfiltrate the wrapping key.
    Password-manager-standard "unlock once per device per day" UX
  Effort: medium — needs Lock button + TTL + wrap/unwrap plumbing
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: C. plain localStorage raw key
  Survives: everything, forever
  Exposure: raw key material at rest, trivially exfiltrable
  Effort: — don't

  One honest caveat on B: because our cipher is XChaCha20 (noble, JS-side), the
  raw key must exist in JS memory when in use — so B's advantage over A is
  persistence scope + the non-extractable wrap, not full key isolation. The
  passkey-every-time status quo is strictly the strongest posture; everything
  else is a convenience window.

  My recommendation: A now (refresh is the actual pain; tab-close re-auth is a
  reasonable line for a beta E2EE vault), B as a follow-up decision if you find
  yourself annoyed by new-tab sign-ins too. Want me to file it as a Keeper A.2
  work item (with the A→B framing), to be picked up after rc.20 ships? I won't
  context-switch into it now — the rc.20 gate is mid-flight.

⏺ Major finding for the record — this goes beyond rc.20

  Free-tier OpenClaw writes have been chain-broken since ops-1 (June 5). The
  plugin never adopted the relay's authoritative chain_id from billing — it
  derives the chain from tier with the retired two-tier mapping (free → Base 
  Sepolia). Every successful billing sync flips a free account's chainId to
  84532: eth_getCode then reads the wrong chain (clean 0x → attaches initCode →
  deterministic AA10), and UserOps get hashed with the wrong chainId. The rc.18
  "P2 AA10", the rc.19 #78 saga, and today's clean-process AA10 are all this one
  bug wearing different hats — it only fires when billing works, which is why
  it looked intermittent. Hermes and ZeroClaw consume chain_id correctly; the
  OpenClaw plugin was the straggler (and the compat matrix wrongly says it
  does).

  Task 12 dispatched (adopt chain_id verbatim, default 100, kill the tier
  mapping, fix the ?? 84532 derivation default). After it lands + verifies: qa.4
  tarball → one more reinstall (sorry — third one, but this is exactly what the
  gate is for), then gate 4 should finally show a landed UserOp.

  Meanwhile: your gate-3 recall test on qa.3 is still valid and useful (reads
  don't touch the chain override path the same way — recall worked earlier
  today). Writes will keep AA10-ing until qa.4 is on.

  Also queued for the wrap-up: #402 comment + release-pipeline note flagging
  that rc.19 (current npm rc tag) has broken free-tier writes — one more reason
  it stays NO-GO.
※ recap: Goal: ship a stable OpenClaw rc.20 where recall survives reinstall. Slot fix is verified on pop-os; the agent is now fixing the free-tier wrong-chain bug that breaks writes. Next: I build qa.4, then you reinstall it via Telegram.

⏺ Root cause of the residual failure understood — and it's not a stabilization
  bug:

  - The agent_end path submits facts one UserOp per fact, sequentially (a stale
  Base-Sepolia gas-workaround — comment still cites it), each waiting up to 120s
  for a receipt. Facts 1–3 confirmed in ~20s each; fact 4's op was sent fine
  (no AA10/AA25) but its receipt didn't arrive within 120s → marked failed
  (txHash empty in the message → the literal tx=…). Classic slow bundler
  inclusion; the op may well have mined seconds later.
  - The state machine handles this correctly: unstored facts retry next cycle,
  and dedup/fingerprint absorbs any op that actually mined after the timeout.

  Decision (my call, documented): no rc.20 code change for this — it's
  transient-inclusion noise with a working retry, in a completely different
  class from the bugs we set out to kill. Follow-up to file: revive real
  executeBatch batching on this path (the single-fact workaround is obsolete
  post-ops-1 — one UserOp instead of five would shrink the timeout exposure 5×)
  + consider a longer receipt window.

  Still in flight: the retry-cycle watcher (background), and Task 13 (sentinel
  hardening, Opus agent). Once the remaining facts land on retry, gate 4 is
  closed in full and the soak clock starts.

⏺ Diagnosis confirmed in five minutes — and it reframes the NO-GO:

  The write-path "bug" is the chain_id bug's twin: the plugin ignores the 
  relay's data_edge_address

  - WASM-baked default DataEdge = 0xC445… = production contract;
  CONFIG.dataEdgeAddress env is unset → the plugin always writes to the prod 
  DataEdge, even against the staging relay
  - The plugin never consumes data_edge_address from billing (grep: zero hits) —
  Hermes/Python does, which is why the 2026-07-04 fresh-account staging test
  passed for Python
  - So the QA's fresh-account UserOps did mine (SA deployed, nonce advanced —
  the QA saw exactly that) — the events just landed on 0xC445, while the staging
  subgraph watches 0xE7a4 → 0 facts, empty recall, "phantom success"

  What this means:
  1. Production users are fine — on prod, the default address is the correct
  address (that's why every pop-os gate passed and real writes land). This is a
  staging-testability failure + client-consistency violation, not a user-facing
  write loss.
  2. rc.20's S3/S4 confound theory was wrong, but so is "new users' memories
  never land" — they land, on the contract the client was pointed at. The plugin
  just can't be tested on staging.
  3. Side effect to note: both QA runs wrote throwaway test facts to the prod 
  DataEdge (encrypted blobs owned by QA-only SAs — harmless, invisible to users,
  but worth knowing).

  Your call (2× consecutive NO-GO = escalation per your rules)

  My recommendation — Option A: rc.22 = thread data_edge_address from billing
  verbatim (cache + getSubgraphConfig), exactly the Task-12 shape, ~20 lines +
  tests → publish → scoped S3/S4 re-QA (which should finally go green on
  staging) → promote path. Closes the client-consistency gap the chain_id fix
  half-finished, and restores staging testability permanently.

  Option B: promote rc.21 as-is (it's production-correct; CLI fixed; all
  stabilization goals met) and take the data_edge fix in the next train — but
  that leaves the plugin untestable on staging and violates the "E2E validates
  on staging" rule.

  Say "A" and I'll dispatch the fix immediately (Opus, TDD, same pipeline).

⏺ Pedro — flagging per your 2×-NO-GO rule, with my recommendation already in
  motion (stop me if you disagree):

  rc.22 NO-GO — the fix stopped one layer short

  What happened: #462 correctly threads data_edge_address from billing into
  config — but the write path never consumes config's address. The submit code
  calls the legacy address-less WASM encoders
  (encodeSingleCall/encodeBatchCall), which have the prod DataEdge baked in.
  Even the env override changed nothing — QA proved it three ways.

  The kicker: core 2.5.x already ships encodeSingleCallTo/encodeBatchCallTo
  (payload + explicit address), built for exactly this in #366, with docs saying
  "clients pass the authoritative address from billing" — the plugin simply 
  never adopted them. Same disease as everything else this saga has found: the
  client-consistency contract implemented in Python/Rust, skipped in the TS
  plugin.

  Why I proceeded rather than paused: the failure isn't an approach question —
  it's a two-call-site adoption gap of an API that already exists. Task 16 is
  dispatched (Opus, TDD — the RED test asserts the staging address bytes
  actually appear in the produced calldata, so this can't stop-short again).
  Then rc.23 → same staging gate.

  Never at risk: prod users. Every one of these NO-GOs was staging-testability;
  prod writes always went to the right contract.

  Also two QA-surfaced runbook items now on #402: openclaw plugins install @…@rc
  silently no-ops when the same tag is installed (deploy path needs
  uninstall+cache-clear), and the plugin's stored=N, failed=0 success message
  should be gated on a confirmed read from the configured subgraph.

  Say the word if you'd rather handle rc.23 differently; otherwise the pipeline
  runs: Task 16 PR → verify → merge → rc.23 → QA gate → your promote.

  effort parameter (only the Workflow tool takes per-stage effort). So the
  subagents run at whatever effort their agent definition / inherited session
  setting carries — I can pin the model to Opus 4.8 but not force "high" on an
  Agent dispatch. If you want guaranteed Opus-4.8-high for implementation, a
  Workflow (which does take effort: 'high' per stage) is the only way to set
  both explicitly; the trade-off is less back-and-forth than my current
  spawn-and-review loop.

  ANTHROPIC endpoint routes to z.ai/GLM, and that Agent subagents inherit the
  session provider — so "opus" as a model name may resolve to a GLM model rather
  than true Anthropic Opus 4.8. I requested Opus 4.8; I can't independently

  - Model: yes, I control it — I'll pin opus on every Agent dispatch (the two
  running now, mcp439 and the review, were both dispatched that way).
  - Effort: the Agent tool has no per-call effort knob — it exposes model but
  not effort, so a spawned subagent inherits the session effort. To guarantee
  "medium," the reliable levers are (a) keep the session at medium effort, or
  (b) use a Workflow (its stages take model:'opus', effort:'medium' explicitly).
  For normal spawn-and-review dispatches I'll rely on session-inherited effort;
  if a task needs effort pinned regardless of session, I'll route it through a
  Workflow stage. Flag me if you'd rather I default everything to
  Workflow-with-pinned-effort — it's more overhead per task but removes the
  ambiguity.

  Also worth knowing given the earlier question: because this session's
  ANTHROPIC endpoint may route to z.ai/GLM, "opus" as a label isn't a
  cryptographic guarantee of true Anthropic Opus 4.8 — the dispatch requests it;
  the provider serves what it serves. Your own session is now on Opus 4.8 (1M),

❯ how are you dispatching subagents? which model and effort?                    

⏺ Concretely, for the two I just spawned (mcpqa439, ci489) and every dispatch
  this session:

  Model: I pass model: "opus" explicitly on the Agent tool call → resolves to
  the latest Opus (Opus 4.8, claude-opus-4-8). That's the one knob I directly
  control, and I set it every time.

  Effort: I cannot set it on an Agent dispatch — the Agent tool schema exposes
  model but has no effort parameter. So a spawned subagent runs at the inherited
  session effort, not something I pin per-call. I don't have a reliable readout
  of what that inherited value currently is (you set your session to Opus 4.8
  (1M); the effort that rides with it is whatever your session is on — I can't
  observe it from here).

  So, honestly: model = guaranteed Opus 4.8; effort = inherited, not controlled 
  by me, and not "medium" unless your session is on medium.

  If you want effort guaranteed at medium regardless of session, the only
  mechanism that takes both is the Workflow tool — its stages accept model: 
  'opus', effort: 'medium' explicitly. Trade-off: a Workflow is a scripted
  fan-out (good for parallel/pipelined tasks), heavier than my current
  spawn-one-agent-then-review loop, and less conversational. Options:

  1. Keep Agent dispatches (model pinned to opus, effort inherits your session)
  — simplest, but effort isn't pinned.
  2. Route implementation/QA tasks through Workflow with model:opus, 
  effort:medium pinned — guarantees both, at the cost of the lighter-weight
  review loop.

  Also the standing caveat: this session's ANTHROPIC endpoint may route through
  z.ai/GLM, so "opus" is a request, not a cryptographic guarantee of true
  Anthropic Opus 4.8 — the earlier mcp439 agent self-reported Opus 4.8 but noted
  a GLM-routed model would claim the same.

  Want me to switch the two in-flight tasks (and future ones) to Workflow so
  effort is pinned at medium, or leave them as Agent dispatches?

         +tSubgraphConfig` default of chain `84532` (Base Sepolia, retire
         +d at ops-1) and the **production** DataEdge `0xC445…` — the tie
         +r→`chainId=100` flip was dead code, never threaded into the wri

         + prod DataEdge and were invisible on the staging subgraph; more
         + broadly it was a client-consistency violation vs the Python cl
         +ient + OpenClaw plugin. New `src/subgraph/chain-config.ts` (`re
         +solveChainConfig` / `buildSubgraphOverrides`) resolves both fie
         +lds from `/v1/billing/status`, carries them on `SubgraphState`,
         + and threads them into all 5 managed-write sites via `stateWrit
         +eOverrides`. Default chain flipped `84532 → 100`. The `TOTALREC

         +lidated by a staging write→read E2E: a fresh account's write hi

         +xed on the staging subgraph, recalled back. Sibling of plugin `
         +#402` (chain_id) + `#460` (data_edge_address).                 
       9 +                                                               
      10 +### Changed                                                    
      11 +- **Tool dispatch unified into a single data-driven table** (`s
         +rc/dispatch.ts` + `src/server-setup.ts`). The two parallel self
         +-hosted/managed `switch` statements + per-tool glue are replace
         +d by one router: `TOOL_MANIFEST` (mode-independent tool list), 
         +`SUBGRAPH_POLICY` / `HTTP_POLICY` (per-(tool, mode) cross-cutti
         +ng behaviour as data), and `createCallToolHandler` with handler
         +s injected as pre-bound bundles. Routing order preserved verbat
         +im; behaviour-preserving.                                      
      12 +- **`memory_id` is the canonical id parameter** for pin/unpin/r
         +etype/set-scope; `fact_id` is kept as a back-compat alias so ex
         +isting callers don't break.                                    
      13 +- Handlers take a shared `ToolContext` (dependency-injection bu
         +ndle) instead of per-handler positional/options variance.      
      14 +- Domain restructure: crypto/subgraph/extraction/tools split in
         +to subdirectories; several helpers de-async'd where they never 
         +awaited.                                                       
      15 +                                                               
      16 +### Removed                                                    
      17 +- **`totalreclaw_migrate` tool deleted.** It was already marked
         + `[DEPRECATED] INVOKE WHEN: never` and was non-functional (the 
         +legacy cross-chain migration path it wrapped was retired with s
         +ingle-chain ops-1). No working call site loses functionality — 
         +hence a **minor**, not a major, bump. Stale catalogs that still
         + reference it get the standard tool-removed error envelope.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ There is a good canonical doc — docs/guides/openclaw-setup.md (+ a lean
  agent-executable openclaw-setup-quickstart.md). Here's the canonical user
  flow, and the one prerequisite that actually matters for a from-scratch test.

  Canonical install (what a user does)

  Paste one line into your OpenClaw agent:

  Install TotalReclaw. See
  https://github.com/p-diogo/totalreclaw/blob/main/docs/guides/openclaw-setup.md

  The agent fetches the guide and runs, mostly silently (a healthy install is ~5
  chat lines total):
  1. openclaw plugins install @totalreclaw/totalreclaw → npm latest = 3.3.12
  (the stable you just promoted)
  2. openclaw skills install totalreclaw → the SKILL.md descriptor from ClawHub
  3. On no existing ~/.totalreclaw/credentials.json, it triggers pairing → hands
  you a URL + PIN; you open it in your browser, generate/paste a 12-word phrase
  (never in chat), reply done
  4. Confirms you're on the free tier (250 memories/mo, Gnosis mainnet, E2E
  encrypted)

  Then it's automatic: recall runs through native memory_search/memory_get,
  facts are captured in the background (no per-fact remember tool).

  Note: stable defaults to the production relay — so this creates a real
  production free-tier vault with real on-chain writes. That's the true user
  experience (use the RC-against-staging prompt if you'd rather not).

  The one gotcha that will make-or-break your from-scratch test

  Your OpenClaw host version. Per the Known Gap we logged: OpenClaw 2026.6.11+
  moved shell exec onto paired node hosts, so an agent driving openclaw plugins 
  install via chat has no host exec by default → install-via-chat fails. It
  works cleanly on ≤2026.6.8 gateway-exec setups (that's what the pop-os QA host
  runs). If you're on 2026.6.11+, you'd install via the Plugins UI or a host
  with exec.

  On the patch: agreed, we own this, not upstream. One design constraint shapes
  it — when the slot is memory-core, OpenClaw loads the plugin as disabled, so 
  its register() never runs. That means the plugin cannot self-heal from inside 
  itself (which is also why the rc.20 stabilization deliberately removed the
  in-plugin self-heal — it was fighting a battle it couldn't win). The fix has
  to live in the install/setup flow (the thing that runs before the plugin is
  active), which is the agent's playbook + a recovery tool.

  The robust, non-fragile patch (doesn't re-add the removed poller, doesn't
  fight OpenClaw):
  - Install flow (SKILL.md + quickstart): after openclaw plugins install, always
  run openclaw plugins enable totalreclaw — idempotent (no-op on a clean
  install where native selection already set the slot; fixes the slot on a
  polluted-config reinstall). And on an "already exists" failure, uninstall +
  delete the orphan npm/projects/*totalreclaw* dir + retry. This makes both
  fresh-install and reinstall self-correct.
  - Docs: a troubleshooting section (symptom memory slot set to "memory-core" /
  plugin disabled / pairing 502 → the enable + orphan-cleanup fix).

  That covers the agent-driven path (the canonical one). The open question is
  whether to also add a code recovery command for manual/non-agent users:

      141  
      142  **Plugin BEFORE skill is deliberate** (post-Pedro QA): the ski
           ll install triggers a config-driven SIGUSR1 gateway reload tha
           t can race a concurrent plugin install. Plugin-first lets the 
           plugin land cleanly, then the skill's reload picks it up on th
           e next gateway loop. If you accidentally do skill-first and th
           e plugin install dies mid-flight, just retry `openclaw plugins
            install @totalreclaw/totalreclaw` once — it's idempotent.

      144 -**No extra config steps needed.** The plugin's `register()` au
          -to-patches `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json` with the keys OpenClaw 
          -2026.5.x looks for (`plugins.slots.memory`, `plugins.entries.t
          -otalreclaw.hooks.allowConversationAccess`, `channels.telegram.
          -streaming.mode`, `plugins.bundledDiscovery`, `plugins.allow`, 
          -`plugins.installs.totalreclaw`) and auto-fires SIGUSR1 to its 
          -own PID after the patch, so the gateway in-process restart pic
          -ks up the new keys without any manual restart — the native mem
          -ory hooks register cleanly on the first session post-install. 
      144 +**Bind the memory slot (idempotent):**                        
      145  
      146 +```bash                                                       
      147 +openclaw plugins enable totalreclaw                           
      148 +```                                                           
      149 +                                                              
      150 +Run this right after the install commands, always. On a **clea
          +n** install OpenClaw's native slot-selection already set `plug
          +ins.slots.memory = "totalreclaw"`, so `enable` is a harmless n
          +o-op. On a **reinstall over leftover config** from a prior ver
          +sion (stale `plugins.allow` / `plugins.bundledDiscovery` / `pl
          +ugins.entries` keys), that native slot-selection can silently 
          +skip — leaving the plugin installed but `Status: disabled` wit
          +h the slot stuck at `memory-core`. A disabled plugin's `regist
          +er()` never runs, so the memory tools and the pair HTTP routes
          + are never served and pairing later fails with a 502. `opencla
          +w plugins enable totalreclaw` re-runs the exclusive slot-selec
          +tion and flips the slot (`Exclusive slot "memory" switched fro
          +m "memory-core" to "totalreclaw"`), so it makes both cases cor
          +rect with one idempotent command. See the reinstall note below
          + if `enable` itself reports the plugin isn't found.           
      151 +                                                              
      152 +**What the plugin patches for you (and what it does NOT).** On
          + load the plugin's `register()` writes only the two host keys 
          +it owns — `plugins.entries.totalreclaw.hooks.allowConversation
          +Access = true` (Fix #2) and `channels.telegram.streaming.mode 
          += "off"` if unset (Fix #3) — then fires `SIGUSR1` to its own P
          +ID so the gateway in-process restart picks them up without any
          + manual restart. As of the native-memory line (rc.20, #402) it
          + no longer writes `plugins.slots.memory`, `plugins.allow`, `pl
          +ugins.bundledDiscovery`, or `plugins.installs` — OpenClaw's na
          +tive install owns the slot + allowlist + discovery. That is wh
          +y the `openclaw plugins enable totalreclaw` step above exists:
          + the slot is the host's job now, and the one time it doesn't b
          +ind on its own is a reinstall over stale config.              
      153 +                                                              
      154  > **Setup is a user-initiated pair (native memory integration)
           .** The plugin does **not** auto-pair on load. When no `~/.tot
           alreclaw/credentials.json` exists, the agent triggers pairing 
           via the plugin's in-process HTTP route (`GET http://localhost:
           18789/plugin/totalreclaw/pair/init`, 3.3.14+) — which opens th
           e relay pair WebSocket directly in the gateway process (immune
            to the 30s shell-tool subprocess timeout that killed the CLI 
           path's WS → relay 502) — or falls back to `tr pair --json` via
            `setsid -f`. Either path surfaces the returned `url` + `pin` 
           verbatim, and you complete the flow in your browser (generate/
           import your 12-word recovery phrase browser-side — it is encry
           pted client-side and never enters the chat or the agent's cont
           ext). On completion `credentials.json` is written and `memory_
           search`/`memory_get` work immediately — no manual gateway rest
           art (the plugin self-applies its config + signals `SIGUSR1` to
            reload; if the memory tools don't bind, the agent issues `/to
           talreclaw-restart` autonomously). Recall is native (`memory_se
           arch`/`memory_get`) and facts are captured automatically in th
           e background — you don't call a remember tool per fact.
      155  
      156  > **Auto-extraction safety net.** Even when the `agent_end` ho
           ok is gated by an upstream policy, the plugin runs a filesyste
           m-polling backup that watches `~/.openclaw/agents/<agent>/sess
           ions/*.trajectory.jsonl` every 60 s and runs the same extracti
           on pipeline (NOT a hook event, so it's never gated). The hook 
           fires alongside the poller; offset-based dedup prevents double
           -extraction.

           d onto **paired node hosts**, so the agent can only run `openc

          +einstall over a prior install), do NOT use `--force`.** `--for
          +ce` writes an install record with `version=None`, which OpenCl

          +ge files behind in `~/.openclaw/npm/projects/<hash>/`, and the
          + new install refuses to overwrite them. Clean it and reinstall
          +:

          + # bind the memory slot (see Step 1)                          
      171 +  ```                                                         
      172 +

          +ir. It does **not** touch `~/.totalreclaw/credentials.json` (y

          +or delete anything under `~/.totalreclaw*`. Then verify the ve
          +rsion in Step 2 — it must match the requested build.

⏺ Verified — and your instinct is exactly right. npm allows only ONE trusted 
  publisher per package ("Each package can only have one trusted publisher
  configured at a time"). So if you point it at npm-publish.yml, you'd break 
  promote-rc.yml — your next stable promote would fail with the same E404. Don't
  swap.

  Given the one-slot constraint, here are the real options — pick by how you
  want to spend the next few minutes:

  Option B — token auth for the RC workflow (recommended, durable).
  Keep promote-rc.yml as the OIDC trusted publisher (untouched → stable promotes
  keep working). I switch npm-publish.yml's plugin job back to NODE_AUTH_TOKEN
  (like the core/client jobs already use). You generate a fresh npm granular 
  automation token (publish scope for @totalreclaw) and update the NPM_TOKEN
  GitHub secret — the old one expired 2026-06-22, which is what started this.
  Then: RC = token, stable = OIDC, no conflict, no swapping ever again. I make
  the workflow edit now; you just refresh the secret.

  Option A — temporary swap (fastest, ~3 min, hacky).
  You point the trusted publisher at npm-publish.yml, I publish 3.3.13-rc.1
  immediately, then you swap it back to promote-rc.yml. Works right now, but
  it's fragile — if the swap-back is forgotten, the next stable promote breaks.

  Option C — consolidate (cleanest, later).
  npm-publish.yml already does both rc and stable (via the release-type input),
  so it can be the single publisher for the plugin — register it, and retire
  promote-rc.yml's plugin publish. One trusted publisher, zero conflict. Bigger
  change (the promote would rebuild-from-source instead of re-tagging the exact
  RC artifact), so worth doing deliberately, not under time pressure.

  My rec: B if you can drop a fresh token (durable, ~5 min total), or A if you
  just want rc.1 out this minute and will swap back. Which do you want? For B or
  A I'm ready to dispatch the moment you're set; for C we should talk through
  the promote-artifact tradeoff first.

  Sources: npm trusted publishers docs
